# Benchmark results

The files of this folder are written by [`bench/run.sh`](../bench/run.sh),
one per run, with a header that records the machine, the compiler, the
load and the date; the tables below are those of the run on the AMD EPYC
9654 summarized in the [README](../README.md#performance), turned into
Markdown by [`bench/tables.py`](../bench/tables.py), while
[`bench/summary.py`](../bench/summary.py) gives the summary (with
`--short`, the table of the README). The libraries compared, with the
commit or the checksum of each, are listed in
[`fenchel-20261120-1020.competitors.txt`](fenchel-20261120-1020.competitors.txt).
In each table a row is a structure and a column a case and a size $n$; a
cell is the time in nanoseconds per item for the sorts and the sequences,
and per operation for the other structures, the median of 11 runs, and in
parentheses its ratio to the first row of the table, which is the
structure of the standard library, or that of ADS where the standard
library has none. A row "A/A" is the first structure run a second time,
and its difference from the first row is the noise of the machine; the
largest differences, in the transposes, the FM-indices and the edit
distance, are in cells of a few nanoseconds. Each structure ran in a
process of its own, pinned to core 178 (the parallel ones to cores 184 to
191) with the memory on the NUMA node of these cores, and only while no
other process ran on them (see the README for the details of the checks);
the load of 45 at the start is that of the other programs on the other
node of the machine. The benchmarks keep the code of ADS where a
structure would otherwise run on Boost or on LEMON (e.g., `PointerTable`
and `FlatHashTable`, which a build with Boost puts on
`boost::unordered_flat_set` and `boost::unordered_flat_map`), so that the
rows of Boost are what such a build gets. The files `frangioni-*.tsv` are two earlier runs on an
Intel Xeon Gold 5120, with fewer benchmarks.

- machine: AMD EPYC 9654 96-Core Processor
- cores: 384
- compiler: g++ (Ubuntu 13.3.0-6ubuntu2~24.04.1) 13.3.0
- load: 45.09 23.32 11.56
- date: 2026-11-20T10:20:40+00:00
- core: 178
- runs: 11
- processes: one per structure
- text: words of a Zipf law
- load at the end: 1.80 2.87 2.75
